Allow me to read the draft resolution. [[The Gathered Rise Up]] The resolution of the Polish Sejm on commemorating the memory of Louis Zamenhof on the 100th anniversary of his death. On 14 April 2017, the 100th anniversary of the death of Louis Zamenhof, the creator of the international Esperanto language. Ludwik Zamenhof was born, raised and raised in the multinational community of Bialystok, which inspired him to create a transnational language. He thought that people were divided primarily by a language barrier that causes conflicts and misunderstandings. He believed that the Esperant idea would unite not only the Esperantists, but become the uniting force of all people. The final version of Esperant, known in today's form, was completed by him in 1885. With the increasing popularity of the language of Esperanto, Ludwik Zamenhof, as its creator, gave up all his rights by giving up Esperanto for the benefit of all mankind. In recognition of his contribution to the emergence of a transnational language and its popularization in the world, Louis Zamenhof was awarded the Order of the Legion of Honor in Paris in 1905 and his candidacy for the Nobel Prize in 1913. He died on 14 April 1917. In 2014, by decision of the Minister of Culture and National Heritage, Esperanto was written on the Polish List of Inmaterial Cultural Heritage. On the 100th anniversary of the death of Louis Zamenhof, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland recalls his character and expresses appreciation for his merits and achievements. I ask the High House to adopt a resolution by acclamation. [[Applause]] Thank you. I state that the Sejm adopted a resolution on commemorating the memory of Louis Zamenhof on the 100th anniversary of his death. The formal proposal is submitted by Monika Wielichowska, the Civic Platform Club.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-07-sejm-39-3.u96	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! I'd like to recommend reading Mises to everyone. Ludwik von Mises in the 'Human action', a huge economic volume, distinguished two types of monopolies: a natural monopoly and an artificial monopoly, a state monopoly. When we're dealing with a natural monopoly and there's one and the other pharmacy in a given place, there's always a threat to this entrepreneur that this monopoly will be broken, that someone there will be betting that someone will compete with it. If we have an artificial monopoly, there is no such fear. Then the owner of a company that has an artificial monopoly is lord, God. In fact, the consumer, the citizen does not have any market-based leverage to receive better services. So much for a short economics lesson. In the last term of the Sejm, such small albums were distributed with the audiobook of Louis von Mises. This year is the tragic anniversary of events that are shadowing our history. 77 years ago, after the treasonous attack on Poland, when the Soviet Union occupied our Eastern lands, in February 1940 the first mass deportation took place, and 3 months later - the first mass murder, killing many thousands of Polish prisoners, mainly officers and police officers. The plan for the liquidation of the Polish state, the destruction of intelligence and the destruction of biological substances of the nation carried out during the Second World War by our neighbours was a crime of genocide condemned by the victorious powers, but limited only to the activities of the Nazis. The Soviet war crimes were covered with silence, forgetting that Stalin and his supporters planned the systematic elimination of the Polish element three years before the outbreak of the war. On 13 April 1940 it was the beginning of the execution of Polish officers, police officers and officials who entered Soviet captivity after the fall of the September campaign. Tens of thousands of soldiers were captured in defense battles in the East End. Soldiers were placed in several POW camps: in Kharkov, Katyn, Miednoje and Ostaszkow. At Stalin's personal order, more than 22,000 were launched. officers and representatives of Polish border intelligence, among which were officials of both local government and state administration of various levels, as well as foresters, posters and others. Poland suffered huge losses, losing the limitless intellectual elites as a result of the beastly murders shot in the back of the head. Among the victims of these crimes were also officers 2. The Szwololeer Rokitnian regiment, who were stationed in Starogard Gdansk before the war, went to the eastern part of Poland, where they were captured by the Soviets. Among them there were also others. Colonel Józef Trepto, last commander of the regiment. Col.'s symbolic grave. Józef Trepto is located in an old cemetery where anniversary celebrations take place. Other soldiers were murdered along with him. One of the many victims of those terrible days on this inhumane land was my uncle Joseph Kilian, the priest. reserves. He served in 4. Company of Sick Infantry School, 1. Shooting battalions in Chojnice and 64. The infantry regiment as commander of the platoon. Prisoner Starobielski. An identification plate with his name can be found at a cemetery in Kharkov. It must have been 27 years since the historic event, when the first monument in the world commemorating the victims of the murder of Polish officers in Katyn became the attempt to emigrate independence and British friends at the Gunnersbury Cemetery in London. The construction of the London monument started a series of similar activities. The truth was lied to and silenced for decades after the war. It was only after the Eastern bloc broke up that the world learned what really happened in Katyn and other POW camps. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-07-sejm-39-3.u400	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 15 and 16 March this year. I took part in the conference entitled: The development of multimodal transport in the Elbe and Oder region, which was organised in Brussels. The conference was attended by representatives of Germany, Poland and the Czech Republic and the European Parliament and the European Commission. A representative of the European Commission presented transport corridors and talked about funding opportunities for multimodal platforms. Our government was represented by Mr Jerzy Materna, Secretary of State at the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping. The conference discussed the functioning of water transport in Belgium, Germany and the Czech Republic. Minister Jerzy Materna spoke about a strategy for the development of shipping in Oder. It turns out that inland navigation operates in the countries of Europe, and in Poland it was completely abandoned as a result of the decisions of previous governments. The conference showed the benefits for the economy of the countries of developing this mode of transport in combination with rail and road transport, how river regulation can be used for transport purposes, while developing an eco-system and protecting against floods and at the same time using tourism. The conference coincided with President Andrzej Duda's ratification of the European agreement on major inland waterways of international importance. It is also part of the government's actions announced in the exposé by Prime Minister Beata Szydło, who said: "We want the Polish rivers to become great roads on which they will flow full of barges launched in their native shipyards. And yesterday, the Committee on Public Finance gave a positive opinion on the proposal by the Minister for Development and Finance to allocate funds from the special reserve of the state budget for the construction of Malczyce water level in Oder of PLN 85 million. The construction of this water level offers significant environmental benefits. As a result of the construction of water grades, floodplains are created very valuable naturally. Water collected between water levels has a beneficial effect on the development of water-borne ecosystems, as demonstrated by 80% of the Odrzaska Waterway, once one of Europe's larger transport waters, and all artificial retention shipping tanks in Natura 2000 nature conservation areas due to natural conditions. Other environmental benefits of river regulation for transport are: reducing the number of accidents on wheeled roads, as fewer trucks will be on the road; low levels of noise emissions and air pollution compared to other transports; no need to occupy additional areas for the construction of waterways, as water corridors are designated naturally; the development of waterways is conducive to recreation and tourism, and has an indispensable educational value conducive to actions to respect and learn about nature. No other means of transport fit into the idea of protecting the environment like river transport. I am extremely happy with the money granted to build a water level in Malczyce, because I live in the area of the river Odry and remember from childhood how the shoulders were swimming on it. I hope it'll happen again soon. Thank you.

Poland, on the other hand, declared its readiness to set up a brigade in Estonia. It was also assumed that the British and Romanians were to be stationed in Poland outside the American army. In addition, the Minister of National Defence has initialled an agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and NATO Allied Forces Command. Transformation and Chief Command of the NATO Joint Armed Forces in Europe complementing the protocol on the status of international military command established under the North Atlantic Treaty. Paris protocol - which aims to simplify the rules on which allies operate and their command in Poland. In January this year. ships with equipment arrived at the German port of Bremerhaven. It should be remembered that since the entry of Poland into NATO, such an operation has not taken place on such a scale. 87 Abrams, 144 Bradley infantry vehicles, 18 Paladin self-propelled vehicles, 419 Humvee armored vehicles - a total of 446 track wagons, 907 wheeled vehicles and 650 semi-trailers - came from the United States. This is equipment 3. Brigade of the Armored Forces Battle Team, which is part of the 4th Brigade. The Infantry Division, (Mechanized) stationed daily in Fort Carson, Colorado. A few weeks after tanks and battlecars, helicopters arrived in our country, because armored and mechanized troops must have air support. Aware of this, the Americans decided to take care of it themselves, taking into account the effectiveness of the armed forces and the security of the soldiers. So several dozen machines of three types arrived at the air base in Powid: 10 two-wiring heavy Chinook carriers, 50 Black Hawk helicopters of different versions and 24 Apache helicopters. As part of the first rotation of brigade command and engineering battalions and support, 3. Battalion 29. The Artillery regiment and the 4th. Battalion 10. The Cavalry regiment will be stationed in military facilities in Żagań, Świętoszów, Skwierzyn and Bolesławec. This is a special moment in the history of Poland, which significantly affects the position of the country on the military map of the world. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-07-sejm-39-3.u403	Thank you very much. Mr Jan Mosiński, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-07-sejm-39-3.u404	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! For several years I have been privileged to attend the AGROMARSZ Agricultural and Gardening Exhibition, which takes place in Marszewo, in a very nice village near Pleszewo in Wielkopolska. The organizer of the fair is the Wielkopolska Agricultural Advisory Centre in Poznań. From year to year, the exhibition is gaining momentum. Every year I see growing interest from both exhibitors and visitors. This year we see on the palm of our hand that the living rate of the inhabitants of the Polish village is increasing. We are simply doing what the PO-PSL coalition has not done over the years, leading to growing disparities between the village and the city, disparities in access to cultural and health-social goods. We're equalizing these imbalances. At the official opening of the fair, I drew attention to two important issues. Firstly, the 500+ programme, which is a government support programme by Prime Minister Beaty Szydło, I stress: the government's support by Prime Minister Beaty Szydło, and not by local government - has benefited from more than 60% children from Polish villages and communal-rural centers. This shows that the funds from this program have been specifically addressed. And secondly, the sale of agricultural products is increasing. I would like to remind you that 27 new markets have recently been created, and this has been reported, among others. Mr Krzysztof Jurgel, Minister for Agriculture. Many contracts opening up markets were signed, among others. China. This increase in sales has been highest since 1990. The second, more complicated and complex issue is the labour issue. Thanks to the efforts of the workers of the Agricultural Advisory Centre in Marchów, these fairs are gaining momentum every year. Unfortunately, when voivodship advisory centers were subject to local governments of the voivodship, the coalition of PO-PSL in seymikas was not interested in low salaries of employees of agricultural advisory centers. When I was acting as Councilman of the Sejm of the Wielkopolska Voivodeship, I repeatedly made efforts to allocate funds to increase the salaries of the employees of these centres. Unfortunately, I've always met with the same answer: there's no money for it. During my meeting with the headship of the Independent Self-Governmental Trade Union at the Wielkopolska Agricultural Advisory Centre, Janusz Michałowicz and Jan Kałużny, this issue has again been discussed. At the time, I declared that I would address this issue and, in the near future, I would ask Minister Krzysztof Jurgiel to ask him about any plans to raise salaries for the workers of agricultural advisory centres. Why am I bringing this up? Ladies and gentlemen, first of all, employees of agricultural advisory centres are high-quality specialists with a higher specialist education which allows them to provide high-quality agricultural advice and advice on how to use EU funds. Unfortunately, they earn within the lowest wage. My intention, and not just mine, is that what was neglected by the predecessors, the PO-PSL coalition, should be fixed. This is what workers of agricultural advisory centers deserve both in my constituency, Kalisko-Leszczyńskie and throughout Poland. Ladies and gentlemen, employees of agricultural advisory centers, but also members of the Independent Self-Governmental Trade Union (Solidarity), you have, not only in me, ambassadors of your affairs. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
